Frontend sweep. The storage parts are resolved on `fix/frontend-storage-integration` and `useTransactions` on `fix/frontend-sweep-continued` (§6.29); the fabricated `PendingBilateralRecord.tick` was removed by #977; the store's `seq` is the order the native query returned the rows in, display only. |
| `dsm` · sofi/exercise.rs, sofi/registration.rs, economic/register.rs, economic/native_reserve.rs · `check_*_completion` | The completion-proof verifiers (storage §9 rule 9) have no production caller: no party yet receives a kept proof to check. The two under `sofi` are in the G1 baseline with this reason. |
| recovery boundary | `get_latest_capsule_metadata` counts counterparties through unrelated tables with `unwrap_or(0)` under a "for now … proxy" comment; `recovery_sdk` reads the genesis hash with `unwrap_or_default()`. |

### 6.9 The dead-code pass (branch `fix/beta-skeleton-compile`, 2026-09-24)

Every item-level `#[allow(dead_code)]` in `dsm` and `dsm_sdk` (34) and the SDK's crate-level `#![allow(dead_code)]` are removed. Each item the compiler then named was deleted, or its unread field removed; public items the compiler does not flag were checked for callers by hand where a deletion touched them.

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `dsm` · core/state_machine/transition.rs · `StateTransition`, `create_transition`, `generate_position_sequence` | Reached only from tests. `finalize` claimed to validate commitment integrity and generate signatures: it hashed into a hasher it discarded, looped over the balances doing nothing, and returned `Ok(())`. `sign_forward_commitment`/`cosign_forward_commitment` stored a signature without verifying it. `to_wire_bytes`/`to_canonical_proto` encoded fields no production path set. | Deleted with their tests. The `mod.rs` chain tests apply the operation directly (they only read `transition.operation`); `tools/vertical_validation` runs the same presence check `create_transition` ran, then `apply_transition`. Wire: `CanonicalStateTransitionProto`, `PreCommitmentProto`, `PositionSequenceProto`, `PositionList` removed from `dsm_app.proto`; the frontend bindings are regenerated. |
| `dsm` · types/state_types.rs | `MerkleProof::verify` compared a 32-byte root with the concatenation path ‖ leaf ‖ root, so it could never succeed; `PositionSequence::verify` compared a seed with a seed the caller supplied; `IdentityAnchor` carried an "MPC threshold ceremony" proof. With `MerkleProofParams`, `SerializableHash` and `SerializableMerkleProof`, none had a caller outside its own tests. `State` and `StateParams` held fields nothing read (`positions`, `position_sequence`, `public_key`, `entity_sig`, `counterparty_sig`; `none_field`, `metadata`, `token_balance`, `signature`, `version`, `forward_link`, `large_state`, `previous_hash`). | Deleted, with tests that only assigned a field and read it back. |
| `dsm` · commitments/precommit.rs, core/state_machine/random_walk.rs | `PreCommitment.data` and `RandomWalkConfig.position_count` were written and never read. | Removed. |
| `dsm` · core/identity, crypto/sphincs.rs, merkle/tree.rs, types/operations.rs, core/contact_manager.rs | No callers: `generate_secure_random`, `select_random_subset`, a key type's `sign`/`verify`, `KyberKey::encapsulate`/`decapsulate`, `sanitize_genesis_state`, `copy_subtree_from`, `get_tree_height`, `dec_option_bytes`, `update_chain_tip_with_proof`. | Deleted. |
| `dsm_sdk` · sdk/wallet_sdk.rs | When `Smaster` could not be derived the wallet installed a random Kyber keypair (a key no counterparty's stored copy matches); `device_id_array` fell back to 32 zero bytes on a malformed id; `get_balance` defaulted the token to `"ROOT"`; `core_sdk` was never read; 13 methods and the lock/auto-lock machinery had no production caller. | The wallet is not built without `Smaster`; the router installs the wallet's Kyber key or fails, instead of warning and sending an empty field. A malformed device id is an error; `get_balance` takes the token it reads. Dead methods and the field deleted. No mutation control: the replaced branch needed a genesis hash without a device id in the app state, which no writer produces (`set_identity_info` writes both), and reaching it would take a test seam. |
| `dsm_sdk` · sdk/token_sdk.rs | 19 methods with no production caller; a recipient id was fabricated by hashing whatever the caller passed when it was not 32 bytes. | Deleted; the recipient id must decode to exactly 32 bytes. |
| `dsm_sdk` · jni | `ensure_bootstrap` (two copies), called by 18 entry points, did nothing but log — its comment said so. `route_query_via_ingress`, `route_invoke_via_ingress(_bytes)`, `error_transport_bytes` (two copies) and `jni_catch_unwind_jlong` had no production caller; their tests ran through `ShimRouter`, a router that answered every query and invoke with fabricated success. | Deleted, with `ShimRouter` and the two tests. |
| `dsm_sdk` · bluetooth/pairing_orchestrator.rs | Off Android, `start_ble_discovery` returned `Ok` without starting anything, and `notify_pairing_complete` did nothing. | Deleted. |
| `dsm` · crypto/rng.rs | `init_with_seed`, public in shipped Core, installed a process-wide seeded ChaCha20 behind `random_bytes` and `SecureRng`: one call made every later draw predictable. Its own test called it, so in the serial lib test binary every later test drew from a stream seeded with 42. `ensure_rng_initialization` probed and set a flag nothing read; `generate_deterministic_random` and `mix_entropy` were called only by tests of themselves. | OS entropy only; the seeded mode, its tests, the two generators and the `DSM/det-rng-seed` domain are gone. |
| `dsm` · core/chain_tip_store.rs · `NoopChainTipStore` | Reported every compare-and-set as applied and kept nothing. `BilateralTransactionManager::new` defaulted to it; every caller of that constructor was a test or the validation tool, so those relationships "persisted" tips that went nowhere. | Deleted; the one constructor takes a store. Core's tests use an in-memory compare-and-set store in a test-only module; the SDK's tests use the SQLite store production uses; the dsm integration test and the tool each carry their own. |
| `dsm` · core/bilateral_transaction_manager.rs · `establish_relationship` | The initial-tip compare-and-set result was discarded: a store holding a tip the contact record did not hold was ignored, and the anchor ran from h_0 (the anchor was also recorded before the write). | Refused, before any anchor is recorded. Test: `establish_relationship_refuses_a_store_tip_the_contact_record_does_not_hold` (refusal ignored → red). |
| `dsm` · core/bilateral_transaction_manager.rs · `BilateralPreCommitment` | `target_state_number = hash[0] + 1`, a fabricated counter, was signed into a SPHINCS+ signature made on every BLE prepare and never transported or verified (the protocol signature is `sign_commitment` over the commitment hash); `verify()` compared the struct's hash with a recomputation from its own fields. | The counter, the signature fields and methods and `verify()` deleted; the trace harness no longer checks them. |
| `dsm` · core/state_machine/transition.rs · `create_next_state` | Took a `verification_type` it ignored; the file's `VerificationType` existed only to be converted and passed in. | Parameter, type and converter removed; `apply_transition` passes what it uses, the transfer mode. |
| `dsm` · core/token · `TokenStateManager`, `TokenRegistry` | No production user. `apply_token_operation` (no caller) applied DLV claims and invalidations as no-ops under a comment that "the caller (DLVManager in Phase 5)" moved the balance; nothing does. | Deleted; the file keeps policy-commit resolution and balance keys. |
| `dsm_sdk` · storage/soft_vault.rs | No user. Shipped a public `TestBindingKeyProvider`; leaked its lock file on any write error; discarded the result of restricting the key file to its owner. | Module deleted, with its five domain tags and `SoftVaultExportV1`. |
| `dsm_sdk` · handlers/app_router_impl.rs · `wallet.send` | The durable `submitting` mark the code relies on for crash safety was written with its result discarded, so a failed mark still sent; the post-call status writes and the reconcile mark discarded their failures; a tuple of five locals was kept alive only to silence them. | No send without the mark (the row stays for the reconciliation sweep); later write failures are reported; the unused locals and artifact fields removed. |
| `dsm_sdk` · sdk/token_sdk.rs, sdk/core_sdk.rs, sdk/wallet_sdk.rs, handlers/token_routes.rs, storage/client_db/projection_repair.rs | A failed locked-balance read became 0 and was written into projections; an undecodable projection source hash became 32 zero bytes (or the current state's hash); a lock the row could not hold was discarded; an empty token id became `ERA`; the "per-purpose" locked read ignored its purpose and fabricated a state hash; `TokenSDK::get_balance` took a state hash it ignored and had no caller. | `balance_from_projection` refuses a bad row; pre-advance reads propagate; post-commit writers leave the row to the repair sweep instead of writing a wrong one; an empty token is refused; the locked read is the token's whole lock and says so. Test: `a_projection_row_without_its_source_state_or_with_an_unholdable_lock_is_no_balance` (zero-hash fallback → red; lock refusal discarded → red). |
| `dsm_sdk` · sdk/receipts.rs, handlers/cert_resync_flow.rs, storage/client_db/sender_outbox.rs, bluetooth/bilateral_ble_handler.rs, sdk/wallet_sdk.rs | Bindings and imports kept only to be silenced (`let _ = x`, `_public_key`). | Removed. |
| `dsm_storage_node` · api/infra/hardening.rs, api/infra/network_config.rs, replication.rs | `QUORUM_Q = 2` ("acceptance quorum") and two thresholds "reserved for future use", with no reader; `validate_config`, `create_dev_node_configs` and `detect_network_config` had no caller; `ReplicationManager.local_address` was never read. | Deleted. |
| `dsm_sdk` · tests/ble_smoke.rs | Three tests built a bilateral manager and never used it. | Removed. |
| `dsm_sdk` · sdk/chain_tip_store.rs · `SqliteChainTipStore` | Mapped every outcome other than an applied write to `Ok(false)`, so an `InvariantViolation` (no contact row, malformed persisted state) read as a lost compare-and-set. | An invariant violation is an error; only a parent mismatch is "not applied". `bilateral_reject_tests` had established a relationship over no persisted contact — possible only under the no-op store — and now persists its contact first. |
| `tools/vertical_validation` · `tla-check` | Exited on the TLC verdicts alone: a literal, direct or linked Rust trace that failed to replay printed `OVERALL VERDICT: FAILURES DETECTED` and exited 0, so CI's "TLA+ model checks" step was green over a failing Tripwire refinement. | The exit follows each spec report's combined verdict, the one the report prints. It now fails on Tripwire (see Open: receipt verifiers). |

**Open**

| Location | Finding |
|---|---|
| `proto/dsm_app.proto` | 83 of 486 messages and enums are named nowhere outside the proto (Rust, TypeScript, Kotlin, Swift, scripts), among them `StateWire`, `TransactionParamsProto`, `SensitiveAppDataProto`, the `Kv*`, `StorageObject*`, `BleTransport*`, `SecureHardware*` and `SdkEvent*` families. Some are reached only as a `oneof` arm whose Rust variant name differs; each needs that check before it is removed. A wire-surface pass of its own, with the vector-corpus check. |
| `ci/sofi_reachability.py` (G1) | Reads each file only up to its first `#[cfg(test)]`. 30 production files have code after a test item (`types/state_types.rs` has its test module mid-file); none under `sofi/` today, so no SoFi definition escapes the gate, but a caller written there is invisible and would let a baseline entry outlive its wiring. |
| frontend | `RecoveryPipelineScreen.tsx` (string concatenation) and `SofiScreen.tsx` (`any`) carry lint warnings from #976. Frontend sweep. Resolved on `fix/frontend-sweep-continued` (§6.29): the frontend lint reports no problems. |
| `dsm_storage_node` · main.rs, api/infra/admin.rs, api/transport/gossip.rs, api/infra/hardening.rs | A debug-build dev mode: `main.rs` builds `ReplicationManager::new_for_tests` (an unpinned client, replication factor 1) under `cfg!(debug_assertions)`; admin and gossip auth are bypassed by `*_INSECURE_ENV=1` in debug builds; `enforce_release_safety` returns `Ok` in debug; `scripts/dev/start_dev_nodes.sh` drives it. Removing it changes the local-node workflow — owner decision. |
| `dsm_storage_node` · db/pg.rs · `init_db` | Beside `inbox_spool` (§6.7), `cells` gains columns by `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` and retired tables are dropped at start-up. A clean cut means wiping the live fleet's databases, which bricks incarnation-pinned nodes unless restored — owner decision. |
| `dsm` · types/state_types.rs, commitments/precommit.rs · `min_state_number` | Forward-commitment types carried a state-number counter DSM does not keep. Resolved (§6.11, ruling #4): deleted with the forward-commitment types; nothing in `dsm/src` names it. |
| `dsm` · sofi/wire/objects.rs | Eight `push_bytes` results are discarded on the ground that construction bounds the lengths; the honest form makes those five encoders fallible. |
| `dsm_sdk` · sdk/core_sdk.rs · `apply_incoming_transfer_staged` | A losing racer is told apart from other failures by substrings of the error message. |
| `dsm` · types/state_types.rs · `State::new_genesis` | Flags a genesis state `Recovered`. |
| discarded results (`let _ =`) | After this pass: the offline BLE transport, pairing and `wallet.sendOffline` (dependency boundary), Bitcoin (not touched), the recovery routes (boundary), platform sinks with no host counterpart (the WebView event push, `device.requestAdmission`, the localhost policy), channel sends to receivers that may have gone, and once-cell installs. |

### 6.10 Rulings applied and the legacy transition layer removed (branch `fix/beta-skeleton-compile`, 2026-09-24)

Owner rulings of 2026-09-24 (receipts, `wallet.send`, the rule that nothing which does not contribute to the acceptance predicate may pose as authenticated state), the second false-success audit (R01–R06), relayed findings, and the removal of the `State` transition layer that production never ran.

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| receipts · `dsm` merkle/sparse_merkle_tree.rs, verification/receipt_verification.rs, types/receipt_types.rs; `dsm_sdk` sdk/receipts.rs | Two verifiers that could not check the same receipt; field 11 carried a constant stub; field 9 a second encoding of the path. | One path, one primitive: `verify_smt_replace(pre_root, key, old_leaf, new_leaf, siblings)` folds the parent path with the old leaf to the pre-state root and returns the fold with the new leaf. `verify_receipt_state` is the one set of state rules (sender before signing, recipient before accepting, `verify_stitched_receipt` rules 3–4). Fields 9 and 11 reserved. The SDK builder takes the real tree path and a real Device Tree proof, with no fallback; the SDK's second verifier, its proof (de)serializers and the witness modules are deleted. Test: `the_state_rules_hold_only_for_what_the_one_path_proves` (each mutated field refused; two mutations red). |
| `dsm` · verification/receipt_verification.rs · `verify_stitched_receipt` | Ignored the context's expected parent root, and the context carried a consumed-parent set nothing read. | Rule 2c refuses a receipt over any other root; the unread set is removed (the tracker is the uniqueness rule). Test: `a_receipt_over_a_root_the_verifier_does_not_expect_is_refused` (rule removed → red); a refused receipt consumes no parent. |
| `dsm` · types/receipt_types.rs · `print_test_vector_hash_for_spec` | A test with an empty body. | Deleted. |
| `dsm_sdk` · `wallet.sendSmart` | An empty token id meant ERA; tickers were case-folded at the router. | Refused; the token is named exactly (ruling #5). Test: `a_transfer_naming_no_token_or_a_misspelled_one_is_refused_and_nothing_moves` (mutation red). The `wallet.send` app route is deleted (§6.37). |
| `dsm` · types/policy_types.rs, core/token/policy (R01) | `PolicyCondition::VaultEnforcement` decoded and was enforced against a context no production path built. | The variant, `VaultCondition`, the enforcement context and the `rate_limit` witness removed; the wire field reserved. Test: `a_policy_carrying_a_vault_condition_does_not_decode` (lenient decode → red). |
| `dsm_sdk` · storage/client_db/native_reserve.rs · `release_at` (R03) | A negative stored supply read as zero; generation 0 underflowed. | Errors. Test: `a_corrupt_memo_row_is_an_error_not_a_release` (mutation red). |
| `dsm_sdk` · sdk/token_sdk.rs · `token_exists` (R04) | An unreadable archive read as "no such token". | Propagated. Test: `a_token_lookup_over_an_unreadable_archive_is_an_error_not_absence` (mutation red). |
| `dsm_sdk` · jni/unified_protobuf_bridge.rs (R05) | BLE events other than identity were dropped with success. | Routed to the Android bridge, or `NotReady` without one; an event with no payload is `InvalidInput`. Verified by the Android build only; there is no host harness. |
| `dsm_sdk` · sdk/storage_node_sdk.rs (R06) | A malformed `custom_ca_certs` entry was skipped. | A missing key is none; any other type is an error. Test: `custom_ca_certs_is_a_list_of_readable_paths_or_an_error` (mutation red). |
| `dsm_sdk` · sdk/dlv_pre_commitment_sdk.rs | A public verifier that accepted no signatures, and a hash that did not separate parameter sets. | Module deleted (no production caller). |
| `dsm_sdk` · handlers/token_routes.rs · `token.forget`; sdk/core_sdk.rs · `execute` | A missing device head read as a zero balance; the policy check ran over a zero state hash when no head was loaded. | Both refuse. The router is built only over a head and a head is never cleared, so neither branch is reachable on a live router and no test can build it. |
| `dsm` · types/operations.rs, types/general.rs | `Ops::validate` passed every variant but three; `Ops::execute` executed nothing; `GenericOps`, and every type in `types::general` (`KeyPair`, `IdToken`, `DirectoryEntry`, `Commitment`, `VerificationResult`, `SecurityLevel`), had no user. | Deleted. |
| `dsm_sdk` · contacts `Bricked` | Three send/prepare gates and a send-status arm read a status nothing ever wrote (`brick_contact` had no caller since the initial commit). Tripwire (§53) is enforced by the relationship-leaf compare-and-set. | Removed (owner, 2026-09-24). |
| `dsm` · core/state_machine/transition.rs, relationship.rs, bilateral.rs | The `State` transition layer (`create_next_state`, `apply_transition`, `enforce_operation_authorization` — a presence check — `verify_transition_integrity`, `verify_token_balance_consistency`, the burn and DLV signature checks, `RelationshipManager`, `BilateralStateManager`) had no production caller: production advances through `DeviceState::advance`. R02's owner-key check lived here. | Deleted. The one signing rule is `Operation::signing_bytes`. Production burns are authorized by the policy's `TokenAuthority` condition over `token_authorization_preimage`; `TokenSDK` burns, which signed a preimage only the deleted path read, now carry the same witness as `token.burn` (`signing_authority::token_authorization_witness`, shared with `token.create`). |
| `dsm` · core/bilateral_transaction_manager.rs | Precommitments were hashed over per-pair bootstrap `State`s that nothing advanced, carrying `min_state_number`; `verify_relationship_integrity` recomputed `mutual_anchor_hash` from the same struct's fields; `commit_bilateral_smt_update` and `update_anchor_from_replace*` had no production caller (the trace committed through them over a fresh empty tree); `tx_hash`, `BilateralTransactionResult`, `is_synchronized` and the anchor's `smt_proof` had no reader. | A precommitment is `H(DSM/bilateral-session; lp(h_n) ‖ lp(op))` with its `parent_tip`; the rest deleted; `remove_pending_commitment` folded into `consume_pre_commitment`. |
| `dsm` · core/contact_manager.rs, types/contact_types.rs; `proto` · `ContactAddResponse` | `LocalSmtVerifier` cached genesis material nothing read and proved chain tips over trees that were never the device's; every production contact carried `chain_tip_smt_proof: None` and an empty `genesis_material`. | Verifier, fields and proof type removed; field 5 reserved; the frontend's never-rendered "SMT proof" row removed. |
| `dsm`, `dsm_sdk` · h_0 | Three copies of the relationship's initial tip. | One public `initial_relationship_chain_tip`. |
| `dsm` · types/device_state.rs · `DeviceState::advance`, a relationship's first step | The first advance wrote `h_0` into the relationship leaf and replaced it in one step, so the step's pre-state root was the root of a tree the device never committed; a verifier holding the sender's committed root refused every first-contact receipt, and the root chain broke across relationships (§12). An SDK test asserted the two roots differ. | Owner, 2026-09-24: a relationship is established before its first step. `DeviceState::establish_relationship` commits its leaf at the `h_0` Core derives, as a root advance that moves no value; a device's own relationship is established at its creation; `advance` refuses a relationship not established and no longer takes an initial tip. The SDK establishes on `contacts.add` (also for a contact an earlier attempt did not establish), persisting the head before installing it; canonical rebuild establishes before a relationship's first preserved state. Tests: `a_relationship_steps_only_from_a_leaf_the_device_committed` (fallback seeding restored → red); `a_first_step_receipt_starts_from_the_root_the_device_committed`. |
| `dsm` · merkle/sparse_merkle_tree.rs · `update_leaf` | Returned `Result` and could not fail; 35 callers carried error paths that could not run. | Infallible. |
| `dsm_sdk` · bluetooth/bilateral_ble_handler.rs · `remove_pending_commitment` | Duplicated `consume_pre_commitment`; its callers discarded the result with `let _ =`. | Folded into `consume_pre_commitment`, which returns what it removed. |
| `tools/vertical_validation` | Traces, properties, adversarial attacks and the throughput benchmark ran the deleted layer through `compat_shim`. The DSM trace replay copied the model's next state — `deviceBalance` among it — into the harness for several actions, so those projections compared the model with itself. Adversarial "balance underflow" tested `u64::checked_sub`; "replay" passed when the replay was accepted. The offline-finality and non-interference traces claimed conservation on the struct self-comparison. `adversarial` and `crypto-kat` exited 0 on failure. | Rebuilt on `live_device`: real `DeviceState` heads, SPHINCS+-signed operations, receipts from the sender's advance judged by Core's verifier with a parent tracker. Deliveries in the DSM replay run the real step; the model's balances are projected from real heads through a stated abstraction function; an action the replay does not implement is a named failure, not a copy. Conservation is checked across two heads. Both commands exit non-zero on failure. |

**Open**

| Location | Finding |
|---|---|
| receipt verification on the live path | `verify_stitched_receipt` has no production caller; production checks the state rules with `verify_receipt_state` (the BLE confirm converged onto it, §6.11). Resolved: deleted, and its checks moved onto production's decisions (§6.45). |
| DLV operations | `DlvCreate`/`DlvUnlock`/`DlvClaim`/`DlvInvalidate` signatures were verified only in the deleted `create_next_state`; their executor is `BitcoinTapSdk` (dBTC deferred). No live verification. |
| `dsm` · types/state_types.rs · `State` | A compatibility view synthesized from the head, still read in production (token and wallet SDKs, and the Bitcoin routes, which store `State` in structs and read its `hash` and `token_balances`). Removing it touches Bitcoin code. |
| `dsm` · core/bilateral_transaction_manager.rs · `initial_chain_tip_from_device_ids` | Self-loop relationships derive `h_0` with 32 zero bytes where each genesis goes. |
| two `h_0` formulas | A device relationship leaf starts at `initial_chain_tip_from_device_ids` (the device ids with 32 zero bytes where each genesis goes), the value the online paths and the receiver's A-side pin use; the bilateral manager, the contact record and the BLE symmetric tip use `initial_relationship_chain_tip` (both geneses). One relationship has two `h_0`. |
| `dsm_sdk` · bridge/mod.rs · `AppRouter` | Default trait methods answer "not implemented on this router"; the pre-identity router relies on them. A router without a head cannot advance, and the refusal should say that. |
| `tools/vertical_validation` · DSM replay | Vault and emission actions (`create_vault`, `unlock_vault`, `consume_jap_and_emit`, `consume_spent_proof`, `select_winner`) and the offline actions have no Rust transition in the replay; a trace that reaches one fails on it by name. Harness balances enter by faucet-claim advances without the admission that precedes them in production. |

### 6.11 One receipt on the BLE confirm, headers that name only the sender, no forward commitment, no signed balance anchor (branch `fix/beta-skeleton-compile`, 2026-09-24)

Owner rulings #4 (remove `min_state_number`), #6 (a field no receiver verifies does not stay on the wire) and #7 (remove `Balance.state_hash` from the signed canonical representation), and the ruling of 2026-09-24 on local answers (a response this device's Core or SDK gives its own caller makes no sender claim and carries no headers).

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `dsm_sdk` · bluetooth/bilateral_ble_handler.rs · `handle_confirm_request`; `proto` · `BilateralConfirmRequest` | The receiver checked the sender's parent and child paths separately against roots the confirm carried beside the receipt (fields 3, 4, 5, 9), never bound those roots to the receipt, and fed the unbound roots to the offline-bearer predicate with a zero fallback. | The receiver decodes the receipt once, refuses one not from this session's sender to this device, and runs `verify_receipt_state` against the Device Tree root it keeps for the sender; the bearer predicate takes its roots from that receipt. Fields 3, 4, 5, 9 reserved. Test: `a_confirm_whose_receipt_does_not_hold_is_refused_before_the_ek_check` (the state-rule call removed → red; the device binding removed → red). |
| `dsm_sdk` · bluetooth/bilateral_ble_handler.rs · confirm and commit | Zero stood in for a missing receiver challenge, root, parent tip and policy id; an unresolved receiver policy commit became empty deltas left for the conservation guard to refuse; unreadable cert-chain heads, anchor frontiers and contact Device Tree roots read as absent (an unreadable frontier meant "first transfer, adopt the release's root"); the sender's commit, lacking a signed receipt, archived one rebuilt without signatures. | Each is a refusal or an error naming what is missing. The sender refuses to commit without the receiver's counter-signed receipt; it checks that receipt's state rules against the Device Tree root it keeps for the receiver. |
| `dsm_sdk` · sdk/receipts.rs · `build_bilateral_receipt_with_smt`; storage/client_db/contacts.rs · `get_contact_device_tree_root` | The builder returned `None` with the reason only logged; the Device Tree root read turned a closed database, a malformed id and a corrupt blob into "no root". The local Device Tree commitment was derived from the device id when the app state held none. | `Result` on both; the local commitment comes from the app state or is an error. |
| `proto` · `Headers.chain_tip` (2), `Headers.seq` (4) | The tip was zero from the SDK, the relationship's tip on the b0x path, and a hash of an error string from the Core bridge; the one reader, the previous-tip route filter, compared it with the held tip. `seq` counted nothing a receiver read. | Reserved; the parser refuses both tags and requires `genesis_hash`. The inbox address hashes the relationship's tip (storage node spec §8), so a previous-tip route item is non-adjacent by its route: never applied, consumed when already accepted. The SDK's header counter is removed. Tests: `strict_decode_refuses_the_reserved_header_tags`, `strict_decode_requires_the_genesis_hash` (each gate removed → red). |
| `proto` · `Invoke.pre_state_hash` (4), `Invoke.post_state_hash` (5); `OnlineTransferRequest.chain_tip` (8) | Written as zeros, or read into a `B0xEntry` field nothing read. | Reserved; `B0xEntry.sender_chain_tip`/`next_chain_tip` and `B0xSubmissionParams.next_chain_tip` removed; `wallet.sendSmart` no longer resolves a tip only that field carried. |

Owner rulings #2 (no debug-build bypasses; dev nodes run the production path with a generated local CA, certificates and tokens) and #3 (an explicit schema version, exact-match startup, fresh databases only, no in-place migration; schema version, protocol version and register incarnation are separate axes).

A database without a version marker was treated as adoptable; the local test database held three tables no current code creates. The unwritten `objects` table fed a legacy Device Tree fallback read. | `SCHEMA_VERSION` 1: an empty database is created at it in one transaction with its version row; a database at it must hold exactly `SCHEMA_LAYOUT` and `SCHEMA_INDEXES`; tables without a version, another version, or a drifted layout are refused. The backfill, the `objects` table, its fallback read and its hash domain removed. Tests: `an_empty_database_is_created_at_the_schema_version`, `a_database_with_tables_and_no_version_is_refused`, `a_database_at_another_version_is_refused`, `a_database_whose_layout_is_not_the_versions_is_refused` (each check removed → red). |

Tests: `with_no_admin_token_configured_no_one_is_admitted`, `gossip_admits_only_the_configured_token_holder` (opt-out restored → red). |

Test: `route_chain::tests::a_byte_commit_backs_a_link_only_when_it_follows_its_parent` (valid → Final; bent `parent_digest` → `LeaderLinkUncommitted`; orphan at cycle 2 → not Final). ### 6.15 An unestablished candidate is never read as absence (branch `fix/unestablished-candidate-is-not-absence`, 2026-09-24)

The Lean model had the same skip, and `within_budget_exhausted_is_none` proved it. | `keep_verifying` is `Unavailable` when it kept nothing past an unestablished candidate (a verifying candidate elsewhere is still kept: the identity is the locator). `keep_all_verifying` returns `Discovered::Complete` only when every candidate was examined and established, else `Partial` with what verified. Lean: `keep` and `keepAll` rewritten to match, `within_budget_exhausted_is_none` now requires every candidate established, and `an_unestablished_candidate_is_never_none` and `an_unestablished_candidate_makes_discovery_partial` proved. The Lean mutation (the old skip restored) breaks `kept_verifies` and the new theorem. |

Node-backed tests on Postgres: `an_unestablished_genesis_candidate_is_not_read_as_unpublished`, `a_setup_scan_that_met_an_unestablished_candidate_is_not_a_refusal`. Each gate removed → its test red; each Core flag removed → its test red. |

### 6.17 The signer set authorizes only what the policy's own rules name (branch `fix/signer-set-authorizes-only-what-the-policy-names`, 2026-09-24)

MR-SOFI-0310, SoFi §47 and §49: a policy's signer set and threshold authorize only what the policy's own rules name, and the standard release rule names none; §54: the burn flag governs burns only; Amendment S8: creation is the creator device's.

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `dsm_sdk` · handlers/token_routes.rs · `derive_policy_file`; `dsm` · types/policy_types.rs, core/token/policy/policy_enforcement.rs, policy_validation.rs; `proto` · `TokenAuthorityProto` (oneof 9) | Every token was given a `TokenAuthority` condition requiring the signer set's k-of-n signatures over a witness for burn and creation: a second authorization channel no policy rule names. The burn flag was expressed only for non-transferable tokens; a transferable, burn-disabled token was protected from burns only by that witness. `Burn.proof_of_ownership` and the witness in `CreateToken.signature` existed for that condition alone. | The condition, its preimage, its witness keys, the `(pk, sig)` witness record, `token_authorization_witness` and `insert_auth_witness` are deleted; oneof field 9 reserved. Every token's conditions are its `SupplyCap` and one `OperationRestriction` built from its two flags (`permitted_operations`: `create_token`; `transfer`, `lock`, `unlock` if transferable; `burn` if burn-enabled). `Burn` loses `proof_of_ownership` (a wire change to the canonical operation bytes); `CreateToken` is signed as every operation is, by the device key over its cleared canonical bytes. The signer set stays in the committed blob (§47) and authorizes nothing until a release rule names it. |

A refuted exercise reads nothing more (MR-DSM-0041, MR-DSM-0042). `resolve_trader_position` deleted. |
| `dsm_sdk/src/handlers/node_e2e_tests.rs` | No test drove an in-hand refutation on the nodes. | `dsm_sdk::handlers::node_e2e_tests::a_key_held_by_an_exercise_its_own_bytes_refute_is_skipped_on_those_bytes_alone`: B's own exercise re-aimed at the vault's next generation and signed again by B — one operation's exercise, signed by the trader it names, and refuted by its own bytes (conformance item 7, `LegsDoNotMatchPreimage`: P's legs are not the legs its P(E) derives) — is written final at the next generation's first key by any party; the walk skips it with the nodes asked for the two attempt keys and their ByteCommit material and nothing else (the harness records every request each node serves); B's next trade takes attempt 1 and realizes at the reserves the first trade left. With the in-hand refutation bypassed the nodes are asked for registration cells and objects: red. |
| `dsm_sdk/src/storage/client_db/sofi_vault_head.rs`, `client_db/mod.rs` (schema 19), `sdk/sofi_evidence.rs` | **Found by the test above: a trader's second trade against a vault could not resolve.** The vault head store kept only the head's leaves, replaced wholesale. Resolving the device's own position first walks the vault chain, which classifies the device's own exercise consumed and records the generation it produced; the resolution then needed the leaves of the generation the exercise was built on, the store answered with the head's, the root did not match, and `RouteValidation` was missing a vault leaf every round: `RetriesExhausted` forever. Only a trade at a vault's genesis generation could resolve. | `sofi_vault_leaf` is keyed by generation: every generation this device established keeps its whole tree, and `leaves_at(vault, root)` serves the generation the operation names. `record_resolved_with_conn` derives generation `g + 1` from generation `g`'s set. Client schema 18 → 19 (beta: reset, no migration). With the store answering the head's generation whatever root is asked: red. |
| `dsm_sdk/src/handlers/node_e2e_tests.rs`, `test_support/nodes.rs` | No test produced `RetriesExhausted`. | `dsm_sdk::handlers::node_e2e_tests::a_trade_cut_short_by_a_refused_write_is_the_network_status_until_it_lands`: the harness can make one member's store refuse named cell writes (a trigger on the node's own `cells` table; the put fails whole, the member answers an error, every read serves). The pair refused at its leader: the trade is `RetriesExhausted` at `q` with the position fenced, nothing admitted, no balance moved (since §6.31; until then an error). The pair landing and the first leg's key refused at its leader: `sofi.resolve` is `RetriesExhausted` at `q`, still pending, nothing moved. Every write landing: `Realized` at `q`, balances moved, the head agrees with the admitted root. Mutation controls: §6.31. |

### 6.22 Gates that could not fail (branch `fix/ci-gates-that-cannot-fail`, 2026-09-25)

The shortcut audit of 2026-09-25 ("Gates that cannot fail", "What CI tests is not what ships") re-checked the CI gates. Each row below was confirmed by running the gate in this tree before the change.

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `ci/canonical_bilateral_gates.sh` | Its four checks passed whatever the code did: the allow-globs were passed to `rg` without `--glob`, so `rg` treated `!**/*test*.rs` as missing paths and exited 2 under `2>/dev/null`; the script printed dozens of `PairingState` / `initiate_pairing` hits and then `✓`, exit 0 (run and confirmed). Check 4 reported `✓ Contact existence gating present` for a Kotlin file that does not exist. What the checks banned — the words `pairing`, `EnvelopeV2`, `store_contact…ble` — is not a protocol rule anywhere in `specs/`, and the one real rule (only QR acceptance creates a contact) is held in code, where `store_contact` is unreachable except from the acceptance predicate. | Deleted, with its `ci.yml` step. A word ban is not a gate. |
| `ci/block_bluetooth_mods.sh` | An unconditional `exit 0` that nothing invoked; the BLE README cited a `ci_gates.sh` that does not exist. | Deleted. The README names the gate that exists (`scripts/check_forbidden_symbols.sh`) and states that no gate checks transport timers against protocol semantics: `ci/no_clock_and_no_json.sh` allowlists the BLE transport files. |
| `ci/bridge_contracts_gate.sh` | The strict frontend roots named `WebViewBridge.ts`, which became a folder; `rg` exited 2 on the missing path, `if rg …` read that as "no match", and all five bridge bans passed while the folder was never scanned. | The roots are the folder and `BridgeGate.ts`; every root must exist; `rg`'s exit 2 is a failed scan (exit 2), only exit 1 passes. Run on the real folder: no hits. The gate also runs in the Frontend job now, with `npm run lint`, so a frontend-only change meets it (`ci/layers.toml` never selected `rust-gates` for one). |
| `ci/no_clock_and_no_json.sh` | Exited 0 with a `SKIPPED` line when `rg` was absent. | `rg` absent is a failed gate (exit 1). |
| `scripts/ci_scan.sh` | `fail_if_found` treated `rg`'s exit 2 as a pass, so a rule pinned to a deleted file could never fire: two were (`routing_path_sdk.rs`, `route_commit_sdk.rs`, both gone). | Exit 2 is an error (exit 1); the two rules over deleted files are deleted. |
| `ci/sofi_no_default_evidence.sh`, `ci/admitted_predecessor_readers_fenced.sh`, `ci/sofi_reachability.py` | "Production code" was everything before a file's FIRST `#[cfg(test)]`; a file with an early test item had the rest of its production code never scanned (`formal_infra.rs`: 1,619 of 32,053 bytes). | `ci/production_text.py` removes every `#[cfg(test)]`-attributed item (brace-matched block or `;` statement) and keeps the rest; the three gates read that. Exactly `#[cfg(test)]` is test-only; `cfg(any(test, feature))` items ship and stay. First run over whole files: G1 found `dsm::sofi::signature::verify_signed_object` — the one verifier binding a signed SoFi object's key to an expected signer — has no production caller (the audit's "No production SoFi path binds a precommit's key to the trader it names", MR-SOFI-0151). Baselined by name with that reason; the key-binding change wires it and deletes the line. |
| `ci/sofi_validated_root_constructors.sh` | Counted constructors as literal `-> Self`, so a `Result<Self, …>`/`Option<Self>` constructor was invisible; named `rehydrate_from_admitted_store` as a deliberate puncture and never checked its callers. | Counts all three shapes; enumerates every production caller of `rehydrate_from_admitted_store` (six files, each ruled: this device resuming its own coordinate) and fails on a new one. `sofi_evidence.rs` uses it to label the local device's claim as a foreign trader's (audit, `AcceptedClaim constructable with a caller-chosen identity`); it is listed as a caller and that use is an open finding, not a ruling. |
| `ci/conformance_evidence.py`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` | Nothing ran the gate with `--board-log`, so "Met" was checked as "a test of that name exists"; a binary that crashed before its `test result:` line left its tests counted as passed; a formal property counted as the test a Met row needs. | The main-branch board (`rust-board`) tees its logs and runs the gate on them; a binary without `test result:` leaves every test it started `crashed`; a Met row and a matrix Negative-test cell need a cargo test. Pull requests stay static. |
| `dsm/.cargo/config.toml`, `Cargo.toml`, `deterministic_state_machine/Cargo.toml`, `crates/dsm-android-anchor/Cargo.toml` | `overflow-checks=on` sat in `dsm/.cargo/config.toml`; Cargo reads config from the invocation directory upward, and nothing built from there — CI from the root, Android from `deterministic_state_machine/` and the anchor crate — so integer overflow wrapped silently in every release build and on the board. | `[profile.release] overflow-checks = true` in the three manifests Cargo reads; the unread config file is deleted. |

**Reported, not changed here.** No status check is required to merge (a repository ruleset; the owner's call). The `rust-gates` build and clippy use `--all-features`, which compiles `bitcoin-testnet-bypass` (Bitcoin is parked). `cargo deny` runs only from the Makefile. The Lean job rejects `sorry` and has no axiom inventory. `ci/storage_is_dumb.sh` is a word match; the node routes it does not see (`devtree`, the DLV slot, the genesis forwarder) are a node change, §5A row 5. `ci/sofi_reachability.py` checks column-0 `pub fn` only, and the two completion verifiers in its baseline are the S10 question.

### 6.23 A signed object is recognized only when its signature verifies (shortcut audit, 2026-09-25)

The audit's "Exercise and fulfillment-key recognisers never check signatures" (High, confirmed at `dsm/src/sofi/exercise.rs:52`, `publication.rs:226`, `registration.rs:54`).

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `dsm/src/sofi/publication.rs` · `signed_body`, `recognize_setup`/`recognize_precommit`/`recognize_fulfillment` | Recognition decoded the envelope and copied its signature out unchecked ("Recognition is not verification"). Every reader of a cell or an index — the attempt cells (`exercise_at`), `K_ful(q)` (`names_fulfillment_key`), the locator scans (`keep_verifying`) — took an envelope with any bytes in its signature field as the object. A keyless writer could put a shape-valid exercise first at a successor key's leader and void the honest route that reserved it (rung 5), or stall the vault's ladder with one leader-held copy; a junk-signed `F` first at `K_ful(q)` left the trader's position fenced for good; a junk-signed copy of a trader's setup under its `ρ` made the trader's route Invalid. The test fixtures signed with an 8-byte constant, which passed only because nothing checked. | An envelope is recognized only when its signature verifies over its body's signing digest under the key the body commits (SoFi §9: every object carries its own authority; storage spec §9 rule 3: decidable from the bytes). A signature that does not verify makes the bytes not an object of that kind anywhere. The fixtures sign with the trader's real key. |
| `dsm/src/sofi/exercise.rs` · `recognize_exercise`; `registration.rs` · `names_fulfillment_key` | `F`'s key was never compared with `P`'s at recognition. | An exercise, and a candidate at `K_ful(q)`, count only when `F`'s `(signature_alg, claimant_public_key)` is `P`'s (conformance item 2, decidable in hand). Binding `P`'s key to the trader's identity is not decidable from the bytes and stays MR-SOFI-0151. |
| `dsm/src/sofi/validation.rs` · `setup_valid` | A junk-signed copy of the trader's own setup was Invalid. | Not a setup: not established (`NonVerifyingObject`), never Invalid — one body has exactly one valid envelope, and a copy anyone can publish cannot condemn a route. A setup that verifies under a key other than `P`'s stays Invalid (`NotTheExpectedSigner`). |
| `dsm/src/sofi/signature.rs` module doc | Said the storage member verifies `m_F` at `K_ful` ingress. | The member verifies nothing (storage §9 rule 6); every reader verifies it, through recognition. |

Tests: `dsm::sofi::exercise::tests::an_exercise_counts_only_when_f_and_p_are_signed_under_the_key_p_commits`, `dsm::sofi::registration::tests::only_a_fulfillment_signed_under_a_key_of_this_device_names_the_key`, `dsm::sofi::publication::tests::an_envelope_is_recognized_only_when_its_signature_verifies_under_its_bodys_key`, `dsm::sofi::validation::tests::a_setup_is_the_traders_only_under_the_key_p_commits`, `dsm_sdk::handlers::node_e2e_tests::an_unsigned_exercise_at_a_successor_key_takes_nothing`. Mutations: signature check removed from `signed_body` → the four Core tests and the e2e red; `F` key = `P` key removed from `recognize_exercise` → the exercise test red; from `names_fulfillment_key` → the registration test red.

### 6.24 A reserve release is constructible only for the amount its release rule names (shortcut audit, 2026-09-25)

The audit's "Faucet reserve can be drained by one throwaway-key release" (High, confirmed at `dsm/src/economic/native_reserve.rs:451`).

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `dsm/src/economic/native_reserve.rs` · `release_constructible` | The reserve's construction predicate — the recognizer at every successor cell — checked reserve, parent, generation, set, zero and overdraft, and nothing else. A release of the whole remaining supply (80,000,000,000 at `R_0`), signed by any key the release itself carries, was a recognized object; first at the leader with two more links it was final, `remaining` became 0, and every later claim failed forever. The payout and signer checks ran only later, in the credit arm, after the cell was decided. Honest claimants even completed the attacker's chain for it (storage §9 rule 8). | A `FaucetClaimant` release is constructible only for `ERA_FAUCET_PAYOUT` (`ReleaseRefusal::NotTheReleaseRulesAmount`): SoFi §51 rule 3, units come out only under the conditions the policy commits, and the beta claim policy fixes the amount. An off-rule release is not an object naming the cell, so it never holds one. The conservation tests release payouts only. |

**Resolved by owner ruling (2026-09-25), §6.26.** The release carries the recipient device's `AttA`, and the cell binds its signer to the device it names from the bytes alone; the recognizer never fetches.

Tests: `dsm::economic::native_reserve::tests::a_release_of_any_amount_but_the_payout_never_holds_the_cell`, `dsm_sdk::handlers::faucet_flow_tests::a_release_of_the_whole_supply_holds_nothing_and_the_claim_lands`, `dsm::native_reserve_wire::credits_funded_along_a_lineage_sum_to_what_the_reserve_released` (a credit funded by an off-rule release is refused). Mutation: the amount check removed → the Core test and the e2e red.

### 6.25 Five more unestablished facts that were read as verdicts (shortcut audit, 2026-09-25)

Storage spec §4 (MR-STOR-0021): a fact that is not established is never read as its negation. DSM Amendment A1: evidence that cannot be obtained yet is not a verdict. Each row was confirmed by the audit's verification pass and is fixed with a test that performs the failure.

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `dsm/src/economic/lineage.rs` · `advance_validated`; `provenance.rs`; `peer_lineage.rs` · `step_failure` | The resolver's failure to establish the network's register set carries its class (`Incomplete`, `Invalid`, …); `advance_validated` discarded it for a classless `RegisterNotResolvable`, which the peer walk maps to `Invalid` — permanent and quarantining. An unreadable local catalog branded an honest sender a forger, refused a vault genesis, and cleared the verifier's own peer memo. | `ProvenanceError::RegisterNotEstablished(PeerLineageFailure)` keeps the resolver's class through `advance_validated` and the peer walk (`at_position`). `RegisterNotResolvable` stays for what is decided in hand (unknown network, a candidate that does not re-derive the pinned id). Test: `dsm::economic_admission_lifecycle::a_register_set_not_established_is_not_a_verdict_about_the_claimant`, plus the class assertion in `a_step_failure_keeps_the_class_it_was_established_in`. |
| `dsm_sdk/src/sdk/storage_node_sdk.rs` · `answer` | `204 | 404 => Ok(None)`: a `404` (wrong path, a member without the route) counted as the member taking a put, an index append or a mirror sync, and as a healthy member in `storage.status`. No member route answers `404` on success. | `404` is an error. The `.ok()??` readers already read it as "did not answer". Test on the node's own app: the same member at a path it does not serve refuses all four; at its real path it takes them. `dsm_sdk::sdk::storage_node_sdk::tests::a_member_that_answers_404_took_nothing`. |
| `dsm_sdk/src/handlers/online_finalize.rs` · `verify_acceptance_receipt`; `storage_routes.rs` · `verify_inbound_receipt_sig_a` | `.ok().flatten().unwrap_or_else(root AK)`: a cert-chain head that could not be read was taken as "no head yet", and the chain was checked against the root AK — the fallback the send path forbids. An honest delta was parked as rejected; the recipient's `sig_a` check misreported. The unit test for the projection regression passed only through this path (it ran with no store). | Three-way: a head, genesis (`Ok(None)` → the AK), or an error that is not a verdict. The projection test runs on a readable store. Test: `dsm_sdk::handlers::online_finalize::tests::an_unreadable_counterparty_head_is_never_read_as_genesis` (both verifiers, with the head table unreadable and restored). |
| `dsm_sdk/src/sdk/inbox_poller.rs` · `has_pending_settlement_work`, `stop_poller_for_lifecycle`; `jni` · `onAppBackgrounded`; `inbox.stopPoller` | All five settlement queries were `.unwrap_or(false)`, so an unreadable store meant "nothing owed" and the lifecycle stop killed the poller mid-settlement — including `pending_outbound_replies`, which itself fails closed on a malformed row. The JNI hook also read a panic as "let the host go". | `has_pending_settlement_work` returns `Result`; the lifecycle stop declines on an error; the poller keeps the settlement cadence and logs the cause every cycle; the JNI directive releases the host only on a readable "nothing owed"; `inbox.stopPoller` reports the error. Test: `dsm_sdk::sdk::inbox_poller::tests::a_lifecycle_stop_is_declined_while_settlement_state_is_unreadable`. |
| `dsm_sdk/src/sdk/b0x_sdk.rs` · `retrieve_from_b0x_v2`, `SpoolCoverage`, `RetrievalOutcome`; `storage_routes.rs` · `storage_sync`, `pull_and_process_inbox`; `inbox_routes.rs` · `inbox.pull` | A retrieval in which no member answered returned `Ok(vec![])`, and `storage.sync` reported `success: true, pulled: 0` and counted the run as completed. A read that reached too few members was the same collapse one level up: one member answering empty is not a globally empty inbox, because a delivery lands on the register quorum of members, not on all of them. | A retrieval no member answered is an error. Otherwise it returns a `RetrievalOutcome` carrying its coverage (owner ruling 2026-09-25): `Complete` once `members − quorum + 1` members answered — by quorum intersection every delivery is on at least one of them — else `Partial { responded, needed }`. A sync processes what it read and pushes what it owes, and when any route was read partially or not at all it fails, names the gap, and is not counted as a completed run. `inbox.pull` never answers "empty" from a partial read. Six outage tests asserted `success` on a sync run while the quorum was broken — the collapse itself — and now assert that the sync was incomplete (`test_support::two_device::assert_incomplete`) beside the state they check. Tests: `dsm_sdk::handlers::node_e2e_tests::an_inbox_read_that_did_not_cover_every_delivery_is_not_a_complete_sync` (quorum broken → partial, every member down → no answer, every member back → complete, the completed-run count moving only then); `dsm_sdk::sdk::b0x_sdk::tests::a_read_covers_every_delivery_only_from_the_quorum_intersection_up` (the boundary, 5/3 and 3/2). |

### 6.26 A faucet release occupies a cell only when its signer is the device it credits (owner ruling, 2026-09-25)

§6.24 left the release's signer bound to nothing at the cell: any key could release one payout per generation to a recipient that never credits it, a 100-at-a-time drain that also took each generation from the next honest claimant.

**Ruling.** Carry `AttA`; never fetch in the recognizer. Cell recognition is a deterministic function of the parent and the bytes in hand; a recognizer that fetched identity material would make occupancy depend on availability (the same bytes "not a release" during an outage and "a release" later). The rule is `VerifySig(claimant_public_key, body, σ) ∧ derive_devid(claimant_public_key, AttA) = recipient_devid`. It proves the key is the device's (`DevID = H(AK ‖ AttA)` commits the AK, so same-DevID AK rotation is not a supported operation); it does not prove the device belongs to `recipient_genesis`, which P0–P6 proves when a credit consumes the release. If the invariant must be that anything able to occupy a reserve cell is creditable to the named Genesis, the release needs a self-contained authenticated identity witness — still with no fetch.

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `proto/dsm_app.proto` · `FaucetClaimantRecipientV1`; `dsm/src/economic/native_reserve.rs` · `ReleaseSource::FaucetClaimant`, `release_constructible` | The faucet source carried the signer's key and nothing binding it to the recipient. | Field 2, `claimant_att_a` (32 bytes, a wire change). `release_constructible` refuses a release whose `derive_devid(claimant_public_key, claimant_att_a)` is not `recipient_devid` (`ReleaseRefusal::SignerIsNotTheRecipient`). The TypeScript binding is regenerated. |
| `dsm_sdk/src/sdk/faucet_claim_flow.rs`; `signing_authority.rs` · `current_att_a` | — | The claimant derives its `AttA` from the wallet seed, `G` and its device slot, and refuses to sign a release if its key and `AttA` do not derive its own device id. |
| test fixtures (`native_reserve.rs`, `native_reserve_wire.rs`, `economic_admission_lifecycle.rs`) | Every fixture named a constant recipient device beside a freshly generated key — a pairing no honest producer can make. | The recipient device is derived from the fixture's key and `AttA`. The whole-supply drain e2e signs under a key bound to the device it names, so only the amount rule refuses it. |

Tests: `dsm::economic::native_reserve::tests::a_release_signed_by_any_key_but_the_recipient_devices_never_holds_the_cell`, `dsm_sdk::handlers::faucet_flow_tests::a_release_naming_a_device_its_key_does_not_derive_holds_nothing`. The existing faucet flow (nine tests, each a real claim through the cell) passes with the binding enforced. Mutation: the binding removed → the Core test and the e2e red.

### 6.27 A precommit's key binds to the parent claim it extends (shortcut audit, 2026-09-25)

The audit's "P's key is never bound to the trader" (High, confirmed at `dsm/src/sofi/conformance.rs:488`, `signature.rs:215`). SoFi §17.1 P conformance rules 2, 3 and 8; §20.2 item 1.

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `dsm/src/sofi/conformance.rs` · `fulfillment_conformance`, `parent_binds_the_key` | P verified under the key P itself commits, and item 2 bound F's key to P's: a closed loop. Nothing tied either key to the trader the operation names, so a P naming another trader's `(G, DevID, p)`, with F and every setup signed under any key, conformed. When the parent claim's bytes were in hand as a closure object they were only hash-checked; the claim's key was never read. | Item 1 decides P conformance rules 2, 3 and 8 from the evidence. Single-root parent: the claim at the `claim_ref` P names must verify (`ParentClaimDoesNotVerify`), name P's genesis, device and position and commit `P.void_root` (`ParentClaimIsAnotherPosition`), and be signed under P's algorithm and key (`KeyIsNotTheParentClaimants`). Conditional parent: `C_p` carries no key, so P's key must be the key of the `F` whose `FulfillmentId` P names. That id hashes F's body, key included, so an F that does not re-derive it binds nothing (`ConformanceMissing::ParentFulfillment`). The acquisition fetches it by that id. |
| `dsm/src/sofi/validation.rs` · `validate`; `conformance.rs` | Rule 2's second half, "matches the typed parent reference in `𝒞_E^pre`", was checked nowhere. The producer built every closure empty, so `E` committed to no parent at all. | `ParentClaimRef::validation_ref` is the typed reference. `validate` (the producer's pre-publication check and every route validation) and conformance refuse a P whose parent `𝒞_E^pre` does not reference (`ParentReferenceNotInClosure`). |
| `dsm_sdk/src/sdk/sofi_sdk.rs` · `pre_e_closure`; `sofi_flow.rs` · `exercise_draft` | — | Every draft's closure references the parent P names. The exercise carries the trader's own parent object (`own_closure_objects`), as the completion path already did. |
| `dsm_sdk/src/sdk/sofi_advance.rs` · `own_closure_objects` | Read a conditional parent's `C_p` at `K_root(p)` routed by `P.void_root`, the root at `p`. A position's cells are routed by the root it was built on (`cells_of` routes `F.q` by `P.void_root`), so `C_p` was never found. The path was unreached while closures were empty. | Routed by `own_root_before(p)`: the root this device's admitted position `p − 1` selected. |
| test fixtures (`validation::fixtures`, the conformance rig, `exercise.rs`) | Every fixture named a fabricated parent (`token(0x66)`, a 40-byte string as the "claim envelope"). | The swap and close fixtures sign a real root claim at `P_POS` over the trader's pre-root, and their closures reference it. |

**Open (dependency boundary, not closed here).** A verifier binds P's key to the claim P names and checks that claim against P's own `(G, DevID, p, root)`. It does not establish that the claim is the one registered at `K_root(p)`. A foreign verifier cannot route that cell: it needs the root at `p − 1`, which P does not carry. The trader's own verifier uses its frozen claim. The key → device binding that would close this from the bytes in hand (the `AttA` shape of §6.26) is the audit's K_root recognizer finding, and needs an owner ruling on the claim and precommit formats. MR-SOFI-0144 stays Partial on it.

**Update (2026-10-01, §6.62).** The parent P names is now checked against what the trader's lineage holds at `K_root(p)`, the claim final there and its root, established by this verifier's own admitted position or by the A8 walk. The walk routes the cell from its own authenticated root at `p − 1`. Still open: the key-to-device binding at `K_root` (pre-audit item 1, CORE).

Tests: `dsm::sofi::conformance::tests::a_precommit_under_a_key_its_parent_claim_does_not_carry_does_not_conform`, `dsm::sofi::conformance::tests::a_parent_claim_of_another_position_or_root_does_not_conform`, `dsm::sofi::conformance::tests::a_parent_that_is_not_a_verifying_claim_does_not_conform`, `dsm::sofi::conformance::tests::a_parent_the_closure_does_not_reference_does_not_conform`, `dsm::sofi::conformance::tests::a_conditional_parent_binds_p_to_the_key_of_the_f_that_installed_it`, `dsm::sofi::validation::tests::a_precommit_naming_a_parent_its_closure_does_not_carry_is_invalid`, and the node e2e trades (`dsm_sdk::handlers::node_e2e_tests::a_sofi_trade_executes_end_to_end`, `dsm_sdk::handlers::node_e2e_tests::a_key_held_by_an_exercise_its_own_bytes_refute_is_skipped_on_those_bytes_alone` — a second trade on a resolved conditional parent). Mutations, each observed red on its named test: rule 2 removed from `validate` and from conformance; the single-root key check; each of the genesis, device, position and root checks; the conditional key, position and id checks; the claim's signature check; the producer's closure emptied; the parent F not fetched; `C_p` routed by `P.void_root`.

### 6.28 The placeholder and wiring sweep (#992–#1008, 2026-09-25)

Owner request: find every placeholder or fake piece, and check that everything is connected where it needs to be. Three read-only sweeps (Core; SDK; node, wire and JNI) ran against `8e51f121`, and each High finding was re-read at its source. Owner rulings of the same day: BLE is a transport only; online and offline bilateral are two separate live processes, and the offline protocol is transport-agnostic, decided in Core and run by the SDK; Core derives the SoFi verdict; the preloaded ERA policy is deleted last and leaves its hole.

**Resolved (#992)**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `dsm_sdk` · sdk/inbox_poller.rs · `decode_sync_response` | `storage.sync` answers its caller with a local answer (no headers); the poller decoded it as an addressed envelope, so every cycle read (0, 0) and the poller never announced an arrival. Its tests used a headered envelope production never sends. | Decoded as the local answer it is; the tests build it with `pack_envelope_ok`. |
| `dsm_storage_node` · api/transport/b0x.rs; db/pg.rs | `message_id` was UNIQUE across spools, so an envelope reusing an id was dropped and answered 204; the node decoded envelopes on the way in and out, and one undecodable row stopped a spool read. | The spool stores and returns bytes unopened (storage §8); no uniqueness on the id. Schema version 2. |
| `dsm_storage_node` · api/objects/bytecommit.rs · `mirror_sync` | A failed set-mate reported "0 new". | 204 when every set-mate answered, 409 in no set, 502 when a set-mate did not answer or is not the member configured. |
| `dsm_storage_node` · gossip, discovery, admin maintenance, device-tree mirror, genesis forwarder, DLV slot, timing, hardening | Gossip sent no token and every peer answered 401 while maintenance answered `ok: true`; every node derived the same id from its bind address; the other surfaces had no client, judged content or ignored a re-PUT. | Deleted with their proto messages and config keys. The database requires TLS unless its URL says `sslmode=disable` (a "localhost" substring used to select `prefer`, which falls back to plaintext). |
| `dsm` · core/identity; `dsm_sdk`; wire; Android; frontend | The storage-node MPC genesis ceremony was still in the tree beside the mnemonic-rooted Genesis v3. | Removed everywhere; `genesis_records` holds what the mnemonic path produces (client schema 20). |

**Resolved (#1008)**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| `dsm_sdk` · the Android-target unit tests (jni/helpers.rs, jni/unified_protobuf_bridge.rs); CI · Android Instrumented Tests | They did not compile (21 errors recorded; the last three: a Base32 test matching a `Result` where decode returns `Option`, and a test calling a function its module did not import), and nothing built them. | They compile, and the Android job compiles them with the library's profile and features. |
**Open**

- A step's commitment binds its parent tip and its operation, not its proposer: a direction-less operation (Noop, Generic) proposed by each device on one tip is one commitment, each device answers the other's prepare as its own proposal (with nothing), and both stay Prepared until cancelled. A transfer names its recipient and never collides. Liveness only.
- Terminal session rows are pruned to 25 per counterparty (`prune_terminal_sessions_for_counterparty`). A kept rejection or cancellation pruned before a late copy of its step's frame arrives is decided afresh: a refused proposal could then be held, accepted and never answered. Liveness only; it needs 25 terminal steps with the contact between a frame and its late copy.
- The doors' check and write are one critical section in one process (`STEP_DOOR`); no test forces the interleaving it excludes.
- The frontend `AdditionalDeviceScreen` calls the deleted `device.*` routes; it is the frontend sweep's, and the proto messages it builds stay until then. Resolved on `fix/frontend-storage-integration` (§6.29).
- `message.send` takes its sender and tip from the caller and nothing receives it: build the receive side or delete the feature (owner). Resolved in §6.38: the owner ruled delete (2026-09-28).
- A proposer's cancellation has no frontend control yet: `cancelBilateralByCommitment` reaches the WebView bridge, Kotlin and the handler, and the frontend sweep adds the control. `TxTypeBilateralOfflineRecovered` is no longer produced; its enum value and the frontend badge retire with the frontend sweep. Both resolved on `fix/frontend-sweep-continued` (§6.29).
- `wallet.sendOffline` and `bilateral.prepare` build an operation from UI hints; a BLE event whose token's decimals are unknown carries no display amount; `sync_balance_cache` logs a failed reload; the BLE handler reads the process-global router rather than its own. All four belong to the offline engine (C5a of the sweep plan).
- Nothing runs `dsm_sdk`'s Android-target unit tests on a device; CI compiles them (#1008).
- `dsm_sdk` · bluetooth/anchor_accept.rs · `DsmStateVerifier::verify_transition` answers `true`: Def. 12 step 12 (σ^DSM over the transition and the consumption R_i → R_{i+1}) is not checked inside the offline-release predicate. Since §6.35 the stitched receipt proves the whole consumption — the relationship leaf, the anchor-state leaf the release names (derived from its signed counter pair and frontiers) and the allocation — against the cert's R_i, R_{i+1}; it is still verified by the handler before the predicate runs, so the check rests on caller order. Closing it means carrying the relationship proofs in the release (a wire change in the anchor subsystem); the design is the owner's. `SphincsPart::part_keygen`/`part_sign` answer empty values for trait methods the receiver never calls.
- A device that advances between building its confirm and committing — another relationship's step, a contact added — cannot commit the step: its root is no longer the root the receiver verified, so the step fails and the relationship is held. Nothing holds the head for an open step.
- JNI exports for Bitcoin with no Kotlin declaration (`bitcoinGenerateAddress`, `bitcoinSwapInitiate`, `bitcoinSwapComplete`, `bitcoinSwapRefund`, `bitcoinSwapStatus`, `bitcoinVerifyPayment`): Bitcoin is parked, so they are recorded, not changed.

Tests: `dsm_sdk::sdk::inbox_poller::tests::a_storage_sync_answer_as_the_router_frames_it_is_read`, `dsm_storage_node::api::transport::b0x::tests::an_envelope_reusing_a_message_id_is_kept_after_the_first`, `dsm_storage_node::api::transport::b0x::tests::bytes_the_node_cannot_read_are_kept_and_returned_unopened`, `dsm_storage_node::bytecommit_chain::a_node_in_no_set_refuses_a_mirror_sync`, `dsm_storage_node::bytecommit_chain::an_unreachable_set_mate_fails_the_sync`, `dsm_sdk::storage::client_db::contacts::tests::a_contact_without_its_keys_or_its_tip_is_refused`, `dsm_sdk::storage::client_db::contacts::tests::a_corrupt_stored_tip_is_an_error_not_an_absent_tip`, `dsm_sdk::storage::client_db::contacts::tests::a_device_id_that_is_not_32_bytes_is_an_error_not_an_absent_contact`, `dsm::bilateral::offline::tests::a_peer_is_held_to_the_keys_its_contact_pins`, `dsm_sdk::storage::client_db::token_registry::tests::tampered_policy_bytes_are_an_error`, `dsm_sdk::handlers::wallet_routes::tests::a_token_without_a_registry_entry_has_no_decimals`, `dsm_sdk::handlers::online_finalize::tests::a_receipt_naming_other_roots_than_the_committed_ones_is_rejected`, `dsm::envelope::tests::a_payload_the_proto_defines_passes_the_strict_validator`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::bilateral_ble_handler::tests::a_sender_commit_that_fails_its_checks_settles_nothing_and_holds_the_relationship`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::bilateral_ble_handler::tests::a_commit_ack_for_a_session_this_device_is_not_committing_finalizes_nothing`. Mutations, each observed red on its named test: the addressed decoder restored; decode restored on submit; the id dedupe restored; a partial and a set-less sync answered 204; the Kyber key not required; a short and a NULL tip read as absent; the wire Kyber key not held to the pin; the policy hash check disabled; an unknown token read as 0 decimals; payloads from 107 refused again by the validator; a refused sender commit marked Committed, or not held for reconcile; an ack for a failed or unknown session let through. The stand-in router, once its methods were required, panicked in each of the 31 tests that reached it, which is how the global-router wiring was found. #996: `dsm_sdk::handlers::bilateral_settlement::tests::a_refused_settlement_leaves_the_head_where_it_was`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::offline_steps_commit_on_both_devices_over_a_byte_carrier`; mutation: the settlement outside the advance's transaction. #997: `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::a_sender_whose_head_moved_since_its_confirm_commits_nothing` and the byte-carrier test's commit assertions; mutations, each red on its named test: the sender's root check; each of the step's eight in-transaction writes removed in turn (both heads and both mirrors, the EK step root, the sender's EK step attribution, both session ends). #998: `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::a_receiver_restarted_after_accepting_commits_the_confirm`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::a_sender_restarted_after_its_confirm_commits_on_the_ack`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::a_sender_restarted_holding_its_ack_commits_on_restart`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::a_restored_step_that_does_not_hash_to_its_commitment_commits_nothing`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::bilateral_ble_handler::tests::a_restart_fails_nothing_it_can_take_up`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::bilateral_ble_handler::tests::a_step_in_flight_blocks_the_next_however_old`, `dsm::core::bilateral_transaction_manager::tests::a_precommitment_is_restored_only_as_the_step_it_committed_to`; mutations, each red on its named test: every in-flight row failed on restart; the precommitment not held again; its hash check removed; a held ack not finalized on restart; the acceptance not written; the confirm's row not written in its transaction; the in-flight check removed. #1000: `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::a_lost_ack_is_answered_again_and_the_step_commits_once_on_each_side`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::a_committed_steps_confirm_not_signed_by_its_sender_is_not_answered`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::a_response_delivered_again_is_answered_with_the_confirm_it_owes`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::a_prepare_delivered_again_to_an_acceptance_is_answered_with_its_response`, `dsm::bilateral::offline::tests::a_committed_steps_confirm_is_answered_only_as_its_sender_signed_it`; mutations, each red on its named test: a committed step's confirm left unanswered; answered without the signature check; the response not kept with the acceptance; a repeated response dropped. #1001: `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::a_proposer_cancels_an_unanswered_proposal_and_the_receiver_ends_it_on_its_next_frame`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::a_cancellation_its_proposer_did_not_sign_ends_nothing`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::a_confirmed_step_cannot_be_cancelled`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::a_receiver_that_rejected_answers_the_prepare_again_with_its_rejection`; mutations, each red on its named test: a rejection that ends only the proposer's side; a rejection ending a step unsigned; a cancellation past the confirm; a cancelled proposal answering nothing. #1004: `dsm::bilateral::offline::tests::a_proposal_is_refused_while_another_step_is_in_flight`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::proposals_that_cross_are_each_refused_and_nothing_commits`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::a_step_in_flight_through_one_handler_blocks_the_next_through_another`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::a_committed_steps_prepare_delivered_again_is_not_refused`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::only_a_proposal_awaiting_its_user_can_be_rejected`, `dsm_sdk::storage::client_db::bilateral_sessions::tests::the_step_in_flight_is_read_from_the_rows`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::bilateral_session::tests::the_durable_in_flight_tags_are_the_in_flight_phases`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::an_online_send_waits_for_the_offline_step_in_flight`, `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::offline_step_tests::an_offline_proposal_waits_for_an_online_send_in_progress`; mutations, each red on its named test: the in-flight arm dropped (in Core, and on the byte carrier); the refusal not kept as the answer; the reject guard dropped; the proposer's in-flight check dropped; either door reading one handler's sessions instead of the durable rows; the committed step's answer dropped (red on a restarted receiver, whose memory no longer holds the step); the offline-step clause dropped from the send-ready authority; the online reservation check dropped from the offline proposer's door; `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::stack_tests::the_process_holds_one_ble_stack_per_identity`, red with a stack built on every call. #1007: `dsm_sdk::bluetooth::bilateral_ble_handler::tests::a_refusal_that_cannot_be_recorded_changes_nothing`; mutations, each red on its named test: the hold not required (the refusal answers as recorded); the hold dropped (`dsm_sdk::bluetooth::bilateral_ble_handler::tests::a_sender_commit_that_fails_its_checks_settles_nothing_and_holds_the_relationship`). Formal: TLC on `DSM_OfflineFinality.cfg` 4,481 distinct states clean with liveness; each falsification config red on its invariant and both witnesses reached; `SessionTermination` red locally with the lost-ack re-answer removed, and with the refusal not kept; two steps each way, safety only, 18,192,082 distinct states clean (local, too large for the gate); `DSM_BilateralLiveness` 1,256,040 distinct states clean with its three liveness properties (opt-in set); Lean kernel-checked with `-DwarningAsError=true`, red with each rule mutated into the code's rules and with each invariant clause dropped.

### 6.29 The frontend sweep, storage first (`fix/frontend-storage-integration` #1002, then `fix/frontend-sweep-continued`, 2026-09-25)

Owner request: integrate the frontend with the storage nodes properly, working from the UI toward the backend. Owner rule restated: all logic is in Rust (SDK, then Core); Kotlin carries bytes as the transport and hardware bridge; what comes from hardware reaches Rust before the frontend, and the frontend reaches hardware only through Rust. The frontend renders.

**Resolved**

| Location | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| frontend · services/storageNodeService.ts, config/storageReplicaSet.ts, `dsm_network_config.json` (frontend and `dsm_client` copies) | The frontend kept its own storage node list — six hosts of the retired 2026-03 fleet — with bearer and basic credentials for them in preferences, exported and imported it, passed its endpoints to `storage.nodeHealth`, and placed objects with a JavaScript "Fisher–Yates" over a djb2 hash and mulberry32. None of it was the pinned set (storage §10) and none of it ran in Rust. | Deleted. The frontend holds no node list, no node credentials and no placement; the set is the SDK's. |
| frontend · components/storage/ObjectBrowser.tsx, services/storage/objectBrowserService.ts, objectPreviewService.ts, config/featureFlags.ts | Fetched `/api/v2/object/get/{key}` from the first configured node — a layer skip, to a route no node serves — and showed the bytes without re-hashing them. | Deleted, with the feature-flag module whose only live key was the browser's. |

A member that carries no answer, or a ByteCommit answer without its commit, is refused, never filled in; the screen shows the SDK's own error. |

The dead subscriptions, the flag and its gate, the two emits and the three event types are deleted — and with the gate, `BleContext` itself: a provider whose context nothing consumed since `useBle` went (its scan state and four no-op calls), mounted in `App` for nothing. `wallet.exitCompleted`, subscribed to in three places and emitted by nothing, is Bitcoin's exit flow and is not touched. |
| Kotlin · service/BleBackgroundService.kt, ui/MainActivity.kt, bridge/NativeHostBridge.kt, bridge/ble/GattServerHost.kt, bridge/ble/BleCoordinator.kt, bridge/UnifiedBleBridge.kt, bridge/BridgeBleHandler.kt, bridge/BridgeEnvelopeCodec.kt, bridge/SinglePathWebViewBridge.kt, debug/PairingTestActivity.kt; `dsm_sdk` · bluetooth/pairing_orchestrator.rs `stop_ble_discovery`; `proto` · `BridgeRpcRequest.ble_identity`, `BleIdentityPayload`, `NativeHostRequestKind` 4–7, `NativeHostEventKind` 2; frontend · dsm/transactions.ts `offlineSend`, contexts/ContactsContext.tsx, EnhancedWalletScreen.tsx, dsm/WebViewBridge/ble.ts, dsm/NativeHostBridge.ts, dsm/EventBridge.ts | The frontend ran the radio. Before each offline send it set the identity the GATT server would serve, started advertising and scanning through host requests and slept 1.5 s, swallowing every failure, and restarted advertising when the send finished. The contacts provider set the identity and started advertising on identity readiness and on each learned BLE address — only once some contact already had an address. The wallet screen started advertising on mount and when shown, and stopped it when hidden and on unmount, so an appliance on any other screen, or with no BLE contact yet, could not be reached. Native never started advertising on a cold start: `onResume` ran before the identity loaded, and a permission grant was handed to the UI as the `BLUETOOTH_PERMISSIONS` host event. The pairing loop stopped advertising when it ended. The GATT identity characteristic served whatever the frontend had pushed last. A debug activity with start and stop advertising buttons shipped in the production manifest. | Advertising follows the identity, and native owns it: the BLE service advertises whenever the appliance has an identity, derived again on the service's own worker thread when the service starts, when it binds, when the activity resumes, once genesis or init has produced the identity, when a Bluetooth permission is granted and when the adapter turns on; it stops only with the service. The GATT server reads the identity characteristic from Rust at read time. The frontend sends no radio request: `offlineSend` is one `wallet.sendOffline` call, whose dispatch connects to the peer and scans as it needs; the screen and the contacts provider render and refresh. Deleted: the four BLE host controls (`NativeHostRequestKind` 4–7 reserved), `NativeHostEventKind.BLUETOOTH_PERMISSIONS` (2 reserved), `BridgeRpcRequest.ble_identity` (10 reserved) with `BleIdentityPayload` and the `setBleIdentityForAdvertising` RPC, `stopBlePairingAdvertise`, `BleCoordinator.setIdentityValue` and the caller-less `ensureBleReady`, the GATT host's pushed identity value, and `PairingTestActivity`. The pairing loop stops only its scan. The native lifecycle is compile-checked, not run: a device run is what shows advertising begins on a cold start, after genesis, on a permission grant and on adapter-on. |

A relationship's send status is where Rust already reports it, on the contact list. |
